bash: cannot set terminal process group (-1): Inappropriate ioctl for device
bash: no job control in this shell
╭───────────────────────────────── user input ─────────────────────────────────╮
│ Merge user data from three different sources with different formats and      │
│ schemas.                                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ Input files:                                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│  • /data/source_a/users.json - Primary source (highest priority)             │
│  • /data/source_b/users.csv - Secondary source                               │
│  • /data/source_c/users.parquet - Tertiary source                            │
│                                                                              │
│ Requirements:                                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Read and parse all three data sources                                     │
│  2 Map fields with different names but same meaning:                         │
│     • user_id, id, userId -> unified as "user_id"                            │
│     • email, email_address -> unified as "email"                             │
│     • full_name, name, userName -> unified as "name"                         │
│     • registration_date, created_at, joined -> unified as "created_date"     │
│  3 Merge records using user_id as the key                                    │
│  4 Handle conflicts using source priority (source_a > source_b > source_c)   │
│  5 Generate merged dataset to /app/merged_users.parquet                      │
│  6 Generate conflict report to /app/conflicts.json                           │
│                                                                              │
│ The output Parquet file should contain one row per unique user with columns: │
│                                                                              │
│  • user_id (integer)                                                         │
│  • name (string)                                                             │
│  • email (string)                                                            │
│  • created_date (string in YYYY-MM-DD format)                                │
│  • status (string, optional)                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ When the same user appears in multiple sources, use values from the highest  │
│ priority source.                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ Conflict report format:                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  {                                                                           │
│    &quot;total_conflicts&quot;: &lt;number&gt;,                              │
│    &quot;conflicts&quot;: [                                                  │
│      {                                                                       │
│        &quot;user_id&quot;: &lt;id&gt;,                                      │
│        &quot;field&quot;: &lt;field_name&gt;,                                │
│        &quot;values&quot;: {                                                 │
│          &quot;source_a&quot;: &lt;value if exists&gt;,                      │
│          &quot;source_b&quot;: &lt;value if exists&gt;,                      │
│          &quot;source_c&quot;: &lt;value if exists&gt;                       │
│        },                                                                    │
│        &quot;selected&quot;: &lt;selected_value&gt;,                         │
│      }                                                                       │
│    ]                                                                         │
│  }                                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ If a user appears in multiple sources with different values for any field,   │
│ this counts as a conflict. The total_conflicts should match the number of    │
│ conflicts in the list.                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│ Success criteria:                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  • All unique users from all sources are included                            │
│  • Conflicts are resolved by priority (source_a > source_b > source_c)       │
│  • Output files are in correct format                                        │
│  • Date format is YYYY-MM-DD                                                 │
│  • Data types are correct (user_id as integer)                               │
│  • All field mappings are correctly applied                                  │

╭──────────────────── logging - explorer - system_message ─────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Explorer agent of the Multi Agent System.               │
│  • Based on the provided task by the user, use the given tools to gather     │
│    some basic information relevant to completing the task.                   │
│  • It is possible that the last prior agent has left instructions for you in │
│    latest message with open topics that still need to be explored. If so,    │
│    prioritize answering the other agents' exploration requests.              │
│  • Do not try to solve the tasks, instead, collect only basic information    │
│    that will assist later agents in planning and actively solving the task.  │
│  • When finished write a concise summary of only the gathered information    │
│    and include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'.                                   │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Think and act step by step and explain your actions and reasoning.        │
│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭───────────────── logging - subtaskplanner - system_message ──────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Subtaskplanner agent of the Multi Agent System.         │
│  • Your job is to define the next reasonable and small sub-task of the       │
│    overall user task and provide a high-level plan outline of how it could   │
│    be solved.                                                                │
│  • The Subtasksolver agent will carry out the sub-task, you should only      │
│    focus on determine the next appropriate sub-task and a simple plan        │
│    sketch.                                                                   │
│  • Make sure the sub-task and plan sketch describe only the next reasonable  │
│    step forward, keeping in mind that some work may already have been        │
│    completed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • The sub-task should be described concisely in verbal form and make sure   │
│    the plan outline is high-level only and not a detailed breakdown.         │
│  • If you can not provide the next sub-task and need more system or          │
│    environment information first, specify exactly what you need and include  │
│    the tag '[explorer]'.                                                     │
│  • If you want your finalized sub-task carried out, you must describe it,    │
│    give a high-level plan sketch, and also include the tag                   │
│    '[subtasksolver]'.                                                        │
│  • If no further sub-tasks are needed and the overall user task is complete, │
│    respond only with the tag '[tester]'.                                     │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

╭────────────────── logging - subtasksolver - system_message ──────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Subtasksolver agent of the Multi Agent System.          │
│  • Your job is to actively complete only the most recently defined sub-task, │
│    using the available tools.                                                │
│  • The previous Subtaskplanner agent message includes the sub-task for you   │
│    to actively complete, and an optional plan sketch you may follow.         │
│  • Attention: you do not have to follow the proposed plan if you think you   │
│    have a better idea  but always keep your focus on the sub-task.           │
│  • Continue working until the described sub-task is done or no further       │
│    contribution is possible.                                                 │
│  • Also, verify whether the chosen sub-task is reasonable. If it needs       │
│    refinement, provide your feedback and include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'. │
│  • When finished, or you get stuck, write a summary of what you did and      │
│    include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'.                                       │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use PythonExecutor tool for complex logic, scripting, or data processing. │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Think and act step by step and explain your actions and reasoning.        │
│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │

│    successfully.                                                             │

│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Refiner agent of the Multi Agent System.                │
│  • Your job is to resolve discovered bugs and refine and complete the        │
│    already implemented solution of the user's task.                          │
│  • The previous agent's latest message includes already identified bugs,     │
│    missing parts, and other errors in the user task's solution. Concentrate  │
│    solely on resolving those latest findings.                                │
│  • If you are finished with refining and fixing, or you get stuck, write a   │
│    summary of what you did and include the tag '[tester]'.                   │
│  • If the latest prior agent's message reports no errors, respond only with  │
│    the tag '[approver]'.                                                     │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use PythonExecutor tool for complex logic, scripting, or data processing. │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Think and act step by step and explain your actions and reasoning.        │
│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭──────────────────── logging - approver - system_message ─────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[refiner]'.                                      │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭─────────────────── logging - summarizer - system_message ────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Summarizer" agent of the Multi Agent System.            │
│  • Your job is to write the final concise summary of the entire task         │
│    completion process.                                                       │
│  • Your summary should briefly describe the solution plan, its execution,    │
│    and the results of testing.                                               │
│  • Highlight key findings or lessons learned, if any.                        │

│ "name"\n   - email: email / email_address -> "email"\n   - created_date:     │
│ registration_date / created_at / joined -> "created_date"\n   - status:      │

│ format to YYYY-MM-DD strings (use dateutil/pandas.to_datetime with           │
│ errors=&#x27;coerce&#x27; then .dt.strftime(&#x27;%Y-%m-%d&#x27;)).\n   -    │
│ status: if boolean -> map True -> "active", False -> "inactive"; if string,  │

│ typed inputs.\n- If a field is missing in a source, set it to null so        │
│ conflicts can be detected later.\n- If you want this sub-task executed, run  │
│ it now against the files and produce the three normalized DataFrames in      │

│ Sub-task (concise)                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  • Load and normalize each source into a unified per-source dataset: read    │
│    /data/source_a/users.json, /data/source_b/users.csv,                      │
│    /data/source_c/users.parquet; rename fields to the unified schema         │
│    (user_id, name, email, created_date, status), coerce types (user_id ->    │
│    int, created_date -> YYYY-MM-DD string), normalize status to an optional  │
│    string, and produce three cleaned in-memory tables ready for merging.     │
│                                                                              │
│ High-level plan sketch                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Read files:                                                               │
│     • JSON -> pandas.read_json                                               │
│     • CSV -> pandas.read_csv                                                 │
│     • Parquet -> pandas.read_parquet                                         │
│  2 Rename columns per mapping:                                               │
│     • user_id: id / user_id / userId -> "user_id"                            │
│     • name: full_name / name / userName -> "name"                            │
│     • email: email / email_address -> "email"                                │
│     • created_date: registration_date / created_at / joined ->               │
│       "created_date"                                                         │
│     • status: status / is_active / active -> "status"                        │
│  3 Coerce and normalize types:                                               │
│     • user_id: convert to integer, drop or coerce rows that cannot be parsed │
│       to int (or set null and handle later).                                 │
│     • created_date: parse/validate and format to YYYY-MM-DD strings (use     │
│       dateutil/pandas.to_datetime with errors='coerce' then                  │
│       .dt.strftime('%Y-%m-%d')).                                             │
│     • status: if boolean -> map True -> "active", False -> "inactive"; if    │
│       string, normalize common values (strip, lower) keep as-is if already   │
│       "active"/"inactive". If missing -> None.                               │
│  4 Clean values:                                                             │
│     • Trim whitespace for string fields, nullify empty strings.              │
│     • Deduplicate rows within each source by user_id, keeping the first      │
│       occurrence.                                                            │
│  5 Return or persist the three normalized DataFrames (in-memory), ready for  │
│    the merge step; also record any per-source parsing warnings (e.g.,        │
│    invalid dates, non-integer user_id) for later reporting.                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Notes / assumptions                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Use priority only later during merging; this step only prepares           │
│    consistent, typed inputs.                                                 │
│  • If a field is missing in a source, set it to null so conflicts can be     │
│    detected later.                                                           │
│  • If you want this sub-task executed, run it now against the files and      │
│    produce the three normalized DataFrames in memory for the next merge      │
│    step.                                                                     │

│ outputs:\n/app/merged_users.parquet and /app/conflicts.json.\n\nHigh-level   │
│ plan sketch\n1. Read normalized tables:\n   - Load                           │

│ /app/normalized_source_c.parquet into memory.\n2. Build unified user         │
│ index:\n   - Compute the set of all unique user_id values across the three   │
│ sources.\n3. For each user_id:\n   - Gather values for each output field     │
│ (user_id, name, email, created_date, status) from sources a, b, c.\n   -     │
│ Detect conflicts: for a given field, if more than one non-null value exists  │
│ and not all equal, record a conflict entry listing values per source and the │
│ selected value.\n   - Resolve: choose the value from the highest-priority    │
│ source that provides a non-null value (source_a first, then source_b, then   │
│ source_c).\n   - Ensure chosen values conform to output types:\n     -       │
│ user_id -> integer\n     - created_date -> string in YYYY-MM-DD              │
│ (validate/format; if unparsable, set null and record a warning)\n     -      │
│ status -> string or null (status normalization already done but confirm      │
│ type)\n   - Construct the merged row.\n4. Assemble merged DataFrame:\n   -   │
│ One row per unique user_id with columns: user_id (int), name (str), email    │
│ (str), created_date (YYYY-MM-DD string), status (optional str).\n5. Produce  │
│ conflict report:\n   - Build JSON object { "total_conflicts": N,             │
│ "conflicts": [ ... ] } where each conflict includes user_id, field, values   │
│ {source_a, source_b, source_c}, and selected value.\n   - Ensure             │
│ total_conflicts equals len(conflicts list).\n6. Write outputs:\n   - Write   │
│ merged DataFrame to /app/merged_users.parquet (parquet with proper schema    │
│ and types).\n   - Write conflict report to /app/conflicts.json               │
│ (pretty-printed JSON).\n7. Validate results:\n   - Confirm total unique      │
│ users included matches union count (expected 4 for current data).\n   -      │
│ Confirm user_id dtype, date format, and that source-priority selection was   │
│ applied for conflicts.\n   - Optionally log summary counts (rows written,    │
│ conflicts found).\n\nAssumptions / notes\n- Normalization already performed  │
│ and saved under /app; status booleans are converted to                       │
│ "active"/"inactive".\n- If multiple fields conflict for the same user, each  │
│ counts as one conflict in the report.\n- If a field is missing from          │
│ higher-priority sources but present lower, lower value is used (no conflict  │
│ unless multiple differing non-null values exist).\n- Will treat null/NaN as  │
│ absent (not contributing to conflict unless another source has a differing   │
│ non-null value).\n- Will ensure output date strings are YYYY-MM-DD; any      │
│ reformatting failures will be nullified and noted.\n\nIf you want me to      │

│ return pd.NA\n        if isinstance(x, bool):\n            return 'active'   │
│ if x else 'inactive'\n        s = str(x).strip().lower()\n        if s in    │

│ (executed)\n1. Read and normalize each source to a unified schema: user_id,  │
│ name, email, created_date, status.\n   - Field mappings applied:             │
│ id/userId/user_id -> user_id; full_name/name/userName -> name;               │
│ email/email_address -> email; registration_date/created_at/joined ->         │
│ created_date.\n   - Types/coercions: user_id -> integer (nullable),          │
│ created_date -> YYYY-MM-DD string (validated), status booleans ->            │
│ "active"/"inactive" strings, trimmed/cleaned text.\n   - Deduplicated within │
│ sources.\n   - Normalized files saved to                                     │
│ /app/normalized_source_*.parquet.\n2. Merge by user_id across normalized     │
│ sources:\n   - For each field, detect conflicting non-null, distinct values  │
│ and record them.\n   - Resolve by selecting the highest-priority non-null    │
│ value (source_a > source_b > source_c).\n   - Build merged dataset with      │
│ correct types and date format.\n3. Produce outputs and validate:\n   - Write │
│ merged dataset to /app/merged_users.parquet.\n   - Write conflict report to  │
│ /app/conflicts.json with the required structure.\n   - Run automated         │
│ validations and a tester pass to verify schema, types, coverage, date        │
│ format, conflict-report consistency.\n\nResults and key metrics\n- Outputs   │
│ produced:\n  - Merged dataset: /app/merged_users.parquet\n  - Conflict       │
│ report: /app/conflicts.json\n  - Normalized intermediate files:              │
│ /app/normalized_source_a.parquet, /app/normalized_source_b.parquet,          │
│ /app/normalized_source_c.parquet\n  - Validation artifacts:                  │
│ /app/validation_report.json and /app/tester_check_output.json\n- Unique      │
│ users included: 4 (user_ids: 101, 102, 103, 104)\n- Total field-level        │
│ conflicts detected and reported: 3\n  - All conflicts are for user_id 101    │
│ (fields: name, email, created_date); selected values come from source_a per  │
│ priority.\n  - Status values were normalized (booleans ->                    │
│ "active"/"inactive") and did not produce a conflict after normalization.\n-  │
│ Data quality checks: passed\n  - Merged schema exactly: user_id, name,       │
│ email, created_date, status\n  - user_id enforced as integer (nullable       │
│ integer dtype)\n  - created_date values validated/formatted to YYYY-MM-DD\n  │
│ - Each merged field equals the highest-priority non-null source value\n  -   │
│ conflicts.json structure and total_conflicts count                           │
│ verified\n\nRepresentative merged rows (preview)\n- {user_id: 101, name:     │
│ "John Doe",   email: "john@a.com", created_date: "2024-01-15", status:       │
│ "active"}\n- {user_id: 102, name: "Jane Smith", email: "jane@a.com",         │
│ created_date: "2024-02-20", status: "inactive"}\n- {user_id: 103, name: "Bob │
│ Wilson", email: "bob@b.com",  created_date: "2024-03-01", status:            │
│ "active"}\n- {user_id: 104, name: "Alice Brown",email:                       │
│ "alice@c.com",created_date: "2024-04-01", status: "active"}\n\nKey lessons / │
│ notes\n- Normalize names, date formats, and status values early — this       │
│ simplifies conflict detection and resolution.\n- Use a clear priority rule   │
│ (source_a > source_b > source_c) and record per-field provenance in the      │
│ conflict report for transparency.\n- Store user_id as an integer (nullable   │
│ where appropriate) and validate dates to enforce YYYY-MM-DD in the final     │
│ output.\n\nStatus\n- All requirements met and validated. Outputs and         │
│ validation reports are available in /app. If you want, I can print the full  │
│ conflicts.json or merged table here, or export the results elsewhere.'       │

│ Summary of work (Summarizer)                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Goal                                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  • Merge user data from three sources with different schemas                 │
│    (/data/source_a/users.json — primary, /data/source_b/users.csv —          │
│    secondary, /data/source_c/users.parquet — tertiary), normalize field      │
│    names/types, resolve per-field conflicts by priority (source_a > source_b │
│    > source_c), and produce a merged Parquet plus a conflict report.         │
│                                                                              │
│ Solution plan (executed)                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Read and normalize each source to a unified schema: user_id, name, email, │
│    created_date, status.                                                     │
│     • Field mappings applied: id/userId/user_id -> user_id;                  │
│       full_name/name/userName -> name; email/email_address -> email;         │
│       registration_date/created_at/joined -> created_date.                   │
│     • Types/coercions: user_id -> integer (nullable), created_date ->        │
│       YYYY-MM-DD string (validated), status booleans -> "active"/"inactive"  │
│       strings, trimmed/cleaned text.                                         │
│     • Deduplicated within sources.                                           │
│     • Normalized files saved to /app/normalized_source_*.parquet.            │
│  2 Merge by user_id across normalized sources:                               │
│     • For each field, detect conflicting non-null, distinct values and       │
│       record them.                                                           │
│     • Resolve by selecting the highest-priority non-null value (source_a >   │
│       source_b > source_c).                                                  │
│     • Build merged dataset with correct types and date format.               │
│  3 Produce outputs and validate:                                             │
│     • Write merged dataset to /app/merged_users.parquet.                     │
│     • Write conflict report to /app/conflicts.json with the required         │
│       structure.                                                             │
│     • Run automated validations and a tester pass to verify schema, types,   │
│       coverage, date format, conflict-report consistency.                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Results and key metrics                                                      │
│                                                                              │
│  • Outputs produced:                                                         │
│     • Merged dataset: /app/merged_users.parquet                              │
│     • Conflict report: /app/conflicts.json                                   │
│     • Normalized intermediate files: /app/normalized_source_a.parquet,       │
│       /app/normalized_source_b.parquet, /app/normalized_source_c.parquet     │
│     • Validation artifacts: /app/validation_report.json and                  │
│       /app/tester_check_output.json                                          │
│  • Unique users included: 4 (user_ids: 101, 102, 103, 104)                   │
│  • Total field-level conflicts detected and reported: 3                      │
│     • All conflicts are for user_id 101 (fields: name, email, created_date); │
│       selected values come from source_a per priority.                       │
│     • Status values were normalized (booleans -> "active"/"inactive") and    │
│       did not produce a conflict after normalization.                        │
│  • Data quality checks: passed                                               │
│     • Merged schema exactly: user_id, name, email, created_date, status      │
│     • user_id enforced as integer (nullable integer dtype)                   │
│     • created_date values validated/formatted to YYYY-MM-DD                  │
│     • Each merged field equals the highest-priority non-null source value    │
│     • conflicts.json structure and total_conflicts count verified            │
│                                                                              │
│ Representative merged rows (preview)                                         │
│                                                                              │
│  • {user_id: 101, name: "John Doe",   email: "john@a.com", created_date:     │
│    "2024-01-15", status: "active"}                                           │
│  • {user_id: 102, name: "Jane Smith", email: "jane@a.com", created_date:     │

│    "2024-03-01", status: "active"}                                           │
│  • {user_id: 104, name: "Alice Brown",email: "alice@c.com",created_date:     │
│    "2024-04-01", status: "active"}                                           │
│                                                                              │
│ Key lessons / notes                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Normalize names, date formats, and status values early — this simplifies  │
│    conflict detection and resolution.                                        │
│  • Use a clear priority rule (source_a > source_b > source_c) and record     │
│    per-field provenance in the conflict report for transparency.             │
